This row was written at 18:28 (commit 71855b8), and a LATER MERGE DROPPED IT -- silently. 71855b8 is an ancestor of main by `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, so every commit-level check says the content is there, and it is not: main's file carried 231 rows with three ab_zeroinit rows and no amendment, while 71855b8's carried 232 with it. My own HEAD lost it too, so this was not fb overwriting me -- a three-way merge on an append-only log resolved a concurrent append by keeping one side's lines, and neither side conflicted. Found by reading the merge's own diffstat: `runs/experiments.jsonl \| 1 -`, a DELETION in a file that is append-only and where a deletion is therefore always wrong. This is the recorded 'launch_gate silent merge rollback' shape (a merge quietly dropping a verified fix without a conflict) applied to a ledger instead of code, and the detection rule generalises: after any merge touching an append-only log, the row count must not fall and no line may disappear -- diffstat showing `-` on such a file is the signal.  AMENDMENT (1e's request): state the token count of the reading against the token count the bar was calibrated at, because they differ.  BOTH (3) ARMS RAN 0.328B TOKENS, not the 0.26B a 4-card lane would give: world 5 x batch 16 x accum 2 x seq 4096 x 500 steps = 655,360/step = 0.328B, and both arm logs print '0.33B tok' at step 500. THE BAR WAS CALIBRATED AT 0.20B: ds.seed_variance_0p2b's sigma 0.0516 comes from four 0.2b points (p02_fp32m_s0..s3, each 'mix: 48820 rows = 0.20B tokens scheduled'), so the reading sits at 1.64x the bar's tokens. Four further config gaps between those runs and mine, none of them quantified anywhere: world 7 vs 5, mix_scale_0.2b vs mix_200m_4b, warmup 20 vs 300, fp8 True vs False. Any of them can move run-to-run spread.  THE DIRECTION OF THAT MISMATCH, AND I GOT IT BACKWARDS ON THE FIRST PASS -- recording the error because it is the more useful half. My first version of this note said sigma(0.20B) is probably an OVER-estimate at 0.328B (L(D) flattens, so run-to-run spread shrinks with D), and concluded the 0.24 bar is therefore 'conservative for my reading'. That is inverted. The verdict here is FLAT, which means delta < bar; a bar that is too WIDE makes FLAT too EASY. Borrowing a 0.20B sigma is therefore ANTI-CONSERVATIVE for this verdict -- it can MASK a readable effect, not protect against claiming one. A too-wide bar would be conservative only for the opposite verdict (declaring a move). This is the same trap already recorded in this repo as the v1.10 protocol's v1.6 correction (facts/data_scaling.json, ds.scaling_fit_protocol): 'sigma-hat from the smallest point is an UPPER BOUND on the ladder's sigma, but in the ACCEPT rule an over-estimate LOOSENS the threshold -> false ACCEPT; conservative upper bound was anti-conservative for the verdict.' Whether an error bar is conservative depends on WHICH VERDICT it is guarding, and the same over-estimate is protective for one and permissive for the other.  HOW MUCH IT WOULD TAKE TO FLIP THE VERDICT, so the conditionality is a number and not a worry: the bar is 4.65 sigma (0.24/0.0516), so delta 0.1196 clears it once sigma < 0.0257 -- sigma must roughly HALVE from the measured 0.0516. So: sigma(0.328B) > 0.0257 -> flat stands; sigma(0.328B) < 0.0257 -> the effect was readable and I under-called it. Nobody has measured sigma at 0.328B on this mix, so which side we are on is UNKNOWN, not safe. 'Flat' therefore carries a BORROWED-SIGMA label, and the '+0.1196 = 2.32 sigma' figure in the result field is VOID as a calibrated effect size -- sigma at this token count was never measured. The '0.50x of bar' ratio stands as a ratio against the registered threshold, which is what the pre-registered rule actually asks for.  WHAT DOES NOT CHANGE: the decision, and for a reason independent of sigma. 9 of 9 domains and all 13 readings point the same way, and that way is AGAINST the arm -- so if the effect turns out to be readable, it is readable in the wrong direction for zero_init_out. A change with no upside and a consistent downside sign stays out of the recipe on either side of the sigma question. The seeds still are not worth spending: they would sharpen a cost estimate for a change we are not adopting.  CROSS-RUN RULE (1e, from the world 5 vs 4 retraction): a loss comparison ACROSS runs must name its x-axis and may only be made at equal tokens. Same-step is not same-tokens whenever world size differs. The cmd above cannot have produced this result and never ran: it says --d 1024, and train.py:2023 records that --d is ambiguous under torchrun's arg parsing and exits; it also says NGPU=5 / --batch 16 / --mix data/mix_30b_stage2.json. The result names runs/proberesume_run1.log, whose own header reads 'world 8 ... cfg batch 32 accum 1 seq 4096 grad_ckpt True ... mix data/mix_500m.json', i.e. a different launch on 8 cards with the 500M mix. The cmd is kept verbatim rather than rewritten, because what is wrong here is the pairing, not the text: the numbers in result (peak 69.63 GiB, 12K tok/s/gpu) are real and belong to the world-8/b32 launch, not to any 5-card b16 probe. Anyone citing 69.63 GiB should cite the log, not this cmd. Found by e1 while checking launch lines; corrected by tilerl. | a52e074 |

## What each run taught us

### nan_probe_gc0_nofp8 — 60/60 steps clean, fp8=none, loss 3.616 at step 60 (fp8 arm 3.641)
- **Learned:** The FP8=0 control is also clean, so the fp8 arm's cleanliness is not carried by something unrelated: at this shape neither arm goes non-finite with grad_ckpt off. The two losses track each other (3.641 vs 3.616), which says fp8 was doing arithmetic rather than being bypassed.
- **So:** record eff.fp8_nan_without_grad_ckpt_unreproduced; sft_math.py:207's premise is unsupported AT THIS SHAPE and should not be cited as the reason grad_ckpt defaults True until someone reproduces the original NaN or states the shape it needed

### nan_probe_gc0_nofp8 — exit 0
- **Asked:** Control for nan_probe_gc0_fp8: with grad_ckpt off and FP8 OFF, does the same 60 steps run clean? A clean fp8 arm only refutes sft_math.py:207 if the non-fp8 arm is clean too.
- **Learned:** monitor: process exited cleanly
- **So:** check the log

### nan_probe_gc0_fp8 — 60/60 steps clean, no non-finite tensor; fp8 path Float8Linear (torchao); loss 4.448 -> 3.641, gnorm 6.8-19.6
- **Learned:** With grad_ckpt OFF and FP8 ON, the configuration sft_math.py:207 says goes NaN, 60 steps ran clean on ckpt_pretrain_30b_s2.milestone_keep_b0_step26500 over 64x4096 real pretraining sequences. max_amax -1 is the documented 'not the hand-rolled recipe' sentinel, not evidence fp8 was inactive: the run reports fp8=Float8Linear, so torchao's path was live.
- **So:** run the FP8=0 control before concluding; a clean fp8 arm only refutes :207 if the non-fp8 arm is also clean, otherwise something other than fp8 is carrying the run

### nan_probe_gc0_fp8 — did not run: pod refused the 'cd ... &' shape (166)
- **Learned:** I wrote the launch script to the pod as the rule requires, then still prefixed the command with 'cd /work/aupai &&' inside the backgrounded chain, which is exactly the shape the wrapper refuses. Nothing reached card 4.
- **So:** launch with absolute paths and no cd: setsid bash /work/aupai/runs/<name>.sh > /work/aupai/runs/<name>.log

### nan_probe_gc0_fp8 — did not run: pod: command not found
- **Learned:** harness launch execs the command without the interactive shell's PATH, and ~/bin is not in it, so the wrapper name never resolved. No process reached card 4 and nothing was measured.
- **So:** relaunch with the absolute path /Users/bytedance/bin/pod

### nan_probe_gc0_fp8 — exit 127
- **Asked:** Does FP8 e4m3 backward go non-finite with grad_ckpt off? sft_math.py:207 asserts it and no fact backs it; that default is what makes every MoE checkpoint unloadable by SFT (model.py:1199).
- **Learned:** monitor: process exited 127
- **So:** check the log

### b0_moe_e1 — DIED AT THE FIRST OPTIMIZER STEP, no measurement. Forward PASSED -- params 801.0M, 73.5 GiB/card on cards 1,2, compiled -- then TorchRuntimeError at train.py:737 in muon_update: baddbmm got three (12, 24, 1024, 1024) tensors, expand: the requested shape has too few dimensions (runs/b0_moe_e1.log:231-235). Cards 1,2 released, 0 MiB.
- **Asked:** E1 = 4x total capacity at EQUAL ACTIVE FLOPs and equal tokens, against the dense control ckpt_b0_headmix_armA (same launch line byte-for-byte plus the MoE flags, so scoring is block-paired on identical data order). THE NUMBER THIS RUN PRODUCES: (a) block-paired doc_cu val, arm minus control, at 1B tokens -- adopt at <= -0.010 nat, null at |delta| < 0.003; and (b) expert-load spread over 24 routed + 1 shared experts at w=768, from runs/moe_diag.jsonl every 100 steps. Nobody has either number at 200M active on this backbone. Active FFN is 9,437,184 per MoE layer, exactly the dense FFN it replaces (parity: (top_k + n_shared) * w = 4 * 768 = 3072 = ffn_hidden), so the 3.9x parameter difference is the treatment and FLOPs are held. Code identity: sha256(train.py + model.py) = 7713171da05b150925be92dcec1915814a8eefee1599dfd20bb3485b22bfba24 at main 6640ae12 -- the arm names the code it ran, not a branch tip that moves. Gate shape: LAUNCH_SHAPE_JSON d1024 L12 h8 ffn3072, mix data/mix_200m_8b.json, world 2; arch_tests GO on that shape from runs/launch_tests.json (test_arch_L32 + test_e2e, real_kernel true). SECOND ATTEMPT: the first died before step 1 at 06:52Z on a dtype defect -- autocast covers nn.Linear and not torch._grouped_mm, so the MoE dispatch got fp32 rows against bf16 experts (runs/b0_moe_e1.log:76, row closed fail). Fixed at 19cc2ddd, reviewed by e1 and tilerl. PRE-REGISTERED STOPS, none tuned after seeing a curve: usage fraction < 0.50 at step 300, normalised entropy < 0.70 at step 500, and arm/control tok/s/gpu < 0.85 at step 30 re-read at 100 against the control's 82.3K (steps past 100 are unusable for that ratio -- co-residency, facts/memory_layers.json#mem.m1_throughput_cost). Bias gamma 0.001 and balance alpha 1e-4 are BORROWED from arXiv:2412.19437, not measured here, at 32,768 tokens/routed-expert/step against their 1,966,080 -- 60x fewer, and per SEQUENCE 4x more (512 vs 128). If a stop fires the report says the balancer failed at this scale with the paper's gamma; it does not retune and relaunch. REGISTERED CONFOUND (amendments 6/7): the arm multiplies 75.00% of its FFN FLOPs in bf16 (3 of 24 routed experts per token, raw 3D Parameters torchao never visits) against the control's 100% fp8. Direction favours the arm on readout 1 and penalises it on readout 5, magnitude unmeasured; an fp8-out-of-FFN control is pre-committed if readout 1 reaches -0.010.
- **Learned:** baddbmm is strictly 3-D. Muon.step() buckets by shape and stacks, so a 2-D weight reaches muon_update as (n, out, in) while the MoE experts -- 3-D Parameters (24,1536,1024) and (24,1024,768), one per layer -- reach it as (12, 24, out, in). Every dense parameter is 2-D, so the stack was always 3-D before this arm. THE TEST GAP IS THE REAL FINDING: test_moe_module asserts which optimizer GROUP each parameter lands in and never calls .step(). Group membership and a working update are different claims, and the group assertion was green at 9/9 before the dtype death and 10/10 before this one.
- **So:** Flatten the stacked grad to (-1, M, N), run Newton-Schulz, view back: one batch of 288 expert matrices per bucket instead of 12 batches of 24, and every op there already addresses the trailing two dims. Ruling (f) holds. New scripts/test_muon_3d.py 3/3 -- steps a 3-D param, proves the 2-D path bit-identical (max|d| 0.0e+00), asserts the pre-fix code raises; registered in the hook and on train.py trigger list. E2E_MOE=1 added to test_e2e.py so the full walk covers the arm before the fifth launch.

### probe_noclass — launch proceeds, WARN printed, no class key on the row
- **Learned:** the one-day default restores every pre-existing launch line
- **So:** flip --class to required 2026-09-06

### de_field_probe2 — class=infra-verification, cards=0,1 both on the row
- **Learned:** Both new exp-row fields are written by exp.py start and passed by harness launch. Absent stays distinguishable from empty: no key means unstated, so a metric over history reads null rather than zero.
- **So:** no backfill of the 243 existing rows (4c's ruling): a backfilled class is a guess the metric would then trust

### b0_moe_e1 — DIED BEFORE STEP 1 on the expert dtype, no measurement. torch._dynamo.exc.TorchRuntimeError: meta for aten._grouped_mm refused Expected inputs of BF16 type but got mat_a.dtype=torch.float32 and mat_b.dtype=torch.bfloat16 (runs/b0_moe_e1.log:76).
- **Asked:** E1 = 4x total capacity at EQUAL ACTIVE FLOPs and equal tokens, against the dense control ckpt_b0_headmix_armA (same launch line byte-for-byte plus the MoE flags, so scoring is block-paired on identical data order). THE NUMBER THIS RUN PRODUCES: (a) block-paired doc_cu val, arm minus control, at 1B tokens -- adopt at <= -0.010 nat, null at |delta| < 0.003; and (b) expert-load spread over 24 routed + 1 shared experts at w=768, from runs/moe_diag.jsonl every 100 steps. Nobody has either number at 200M active on this backbone. Active FFN is 9,437,184 per MoE layer, exactly the dense FFN it replaces (parity: (top_k + n_shared) * w = 4 * 768 = 3072 = ffn_hidden), so the 3.9x parameter difference is the treatment and FLOPs are held. Code identity: sha256(train.py + model.py) = 0422c434f8465314fdd995bafb7eb8f4934a83224b81d10aac6894dc0ac7c48c at main 16a738e3 -- the arm names the code it ran, not a branch tip that moves. Gate shape: LAUNCH_SHAPE_JSON d1024 L12 h8 ffn3072, mix data/mix_200m_8b.json, world 2; arch_tests GO on that shape from runs/launch_tests.json (test_arch_L32 + test_e2e, real_kernel true). PRE-REGISTERED STOPS, none tuned after seeing a curve: usage fraction < 0.50 at step 300, normalised entropy < 0.70 at step 500, and arm/control tok/s/gpu < 0.85 at step 30 re-read at 100 against the control's 82.3K (steps past 100 are unusable for that ratio -- co-residency, facts/memory_layers.json#mem.m1_throughput_cost). Bias gamma 0.001 and balance alpha 1e-4 are BORROWED from arXiv:2412.19437, not measured here, at 32,768 tokens/routed-expert/step against their 1,966,080 -- 60x fewer, and per SEQUENCE 4x more (512 vs 128). If a stop fires the report says the balancer failed at this scale with the paper's gamma; it does not retune and relaunch.
- **Learned:** autocast covers nn.Linear and does NOT cover torch._grouped_mm. The model is cast to bf16 and the step runs under bf16 autocast, so the dense SwiGLU takes fp32 activations and returns bf16 for free (both matmuls are nn.Linear) while the MoE dispatch got fp32 rows against bf16 experts. The dense FFN this arm replaces could not show it; the module tests could not either, since they build in fp32 and feed fp32 so mat_a.dtype == mat_b.dtype.
- **So:** Fixed at 19cc2ddd (merged): one cast at the module boundary feeding both the routed dispatch and the shared expert, gate multiply and accumulation kept fp32, out.to(x.dtype) at the return. Reviewed by e1 and tilerl. Check 10 runs under autocast and re-execs model.py with the cast reverted -- without the autocast it went red one line earlier at the router, which would have passed a router-only fix.

### de_field_probe — class=infra-verification landed, cards=None (arg not yet wired)
- **Learned:** The --class field reached the row; --cards was declared but never written into it. Caught by reading the row rather than trusting the flag.
- **So:** wired --cards into the row dict and into harness launch's exp.py call

### b0_moe_e1 — KILLED BY EXACT PID at ~1m20s, no step ran, no measurement. Launched on cards 0-6 at nproc_per_node=7 with one rank on tileRL claimed card 0.
- **Asked:** E1 = 4x total capacity at EQUAL ACTIVE FLOPs and equal tokens, against the dense control ckpt_b0_headmix_armA (same launch line byte-for-byte plus the MoE flags, so scoring is block-paired on identical data order). THE NUMBER THIS RUN PRODUCES: (a) block-paired doc_cu val, arm minus control, at 1B tokens -- adopt at <= -0.010 nat, null at |delta| < 0.003; and (b) expert-load spread over 24 routed + 1 shared experts at w=768, from runs/moe_diag.jsonl every 100 steps. Nobody has either number at 200M active on this backbone. Active FFN is 9,437,184 per MoE layer, exactly the dense FFN it replaces (parity: (top_k + n_shared) * w = 4 * 768 = 3072 = ffn_hidden), so the 3.9x parameter difference is the treatment and FLOPs are held. Code identity: sha256(train.py + model.py) = 0422c434f8465314fdd995bafb7eb8f4934a83224b81d10aac6894dc0ac7c48c at main 16a738e3 -- the arm names the code it ran, not a branch tip that moves. Gate shape: LAUNCH_SHAPE_JSON d1024 L12 h8 ffn3072, mix data/mix_200m_8b.json, world 2; arch_tests GO on that shape from runs/launch_tests.json (test_arch_L32 + test_e2e, real_kernel true). PRE-REGISTERED STOPS, none tuned after seeing a curve: usage fraction < 0.50 at step 300, normalised entropy < 0.70 at step 500, and arm/control tok/s/gpu < 0.85 at step 30 re-read at 100 against the control's 82.3K (steps past 100 are unusable for that ratio -- co-residency, facts/memory_layers.json#mem.m1_throughput_cost). Bias gamma 0.001 and balance alpha 1e-4 are BORROWED from arXiv:2412.19437, not measured here, at 32,768 tokens/routed-expert/step against their 1,966,080 -- 60x fewer, and per SEQUENCE 4x more (512 vs 128). If a stop fires the report says the balancer failed at this scale with the paper's gamma; it does not retune and relaunch.
- **Learned:** A --training launch allocates block_cards, and block_cards was empty, so _allocation_cards fell back to data/mix_scale_run_config.json cards (0-6, the ladder RECORD of what the six mix_scale points ran on). harness printed the disagreement and used the ladder value.
- **So:** block_cards set to 1,2 by 4c at c2cb6466. Cards 1,2 read 0 MiB after the kill; tileRL card 0 job untouched and still running.

### e1_kda_shape_fault — runs/kda_shape_fault.json
- **Learned:** SHAPE REFUTED. All 5 cells PASS on card 5: b1/t128, b1/full-seq, b4/4096 which is the shape that crashed, b16/4096 which is the arms per-rank shape, and b4/chunk64. chunk_kda on random tensors at the crashing shape does not fault, so the per-rank shape alone does not reproduce the misaligned-address crash. The fault needs something this probe omits by design: loaded weights, a cold-vs-warm autotune cache, or the full model call sequence. This is NOT evidence the weights are bad -- the probe can only exonerate, and what it exonerated is the shape.
- **So:** Do not re-run arm_token_corr at batch 4 expecting a different outcome, and do not treat ckpt_b0_headmix_armA or armB as suspect on this evidence. The next discriminator is the autotune cache: re-run the failing arm_corr with TRITON_CACHE_DIR at an empty dir versus the warmed one, which separates cache state from weights without loading two checkpoints.

### e1_kda_shape_fault — exit 0
- **Asked:** the card-7 misaligned-address crash is a SHAPE fault -- the autotuner benchmarking a per-rank shape the arms never ran -- not a model or weights fault
- **Learned:** monitor: process exited cleanly
- **So:** check the log

### mt_6 — ok
- **Learned:** MUTATION-TEST ARTIFACT, not a measurement: a deliberately broken harness.py accepted --cards 6 (RL team) and launched /bin/echo. Recorded because the mutant's success IS the finding -- nothing asserted the refusal, which is why an assertion was then added. No GPU work.

### mt_9 — ok
- **Learned:** MUTATION-TEST ARTIFACT, not a measurement: a deliberately broken harness.py accepted --cards 9 (unlisted)and launched /bin/echo. Recorded because the mutant's success IS the finding -- nothing asserted the refusal, which is why an assertion was then added. No GPU work.

### cardtest_3 — ok
- **Learned:** test of harness launch --cards validation; /bin/echo, no GPU work, no measurement. The row exists because --cards 5 and 3 correctly PASSED validation and the launch proceeded.

### cardtest_5 — ok
- **Learned:** test of harness launch --cards validation; /bin/echo, no GPU work, no measurement. The row exists because --cards 5 and 3 correctly PASSED validation and the launch proceeded.

### e1_arm_token_corr — NO CORRELATION NUMBER. Two independent failures. (1) The job died on the GPU after both checkpoints loaded (card 7 peaked 3061 MiB): torch.AcceleratorError: CUDA error: misaligned address, raised inside fla/Tritons AUTOTUNER while benchmarking kernel configs for its disk cache (triton/runtime/autotuner.py:202 check_disk_cache -> bench_fn -> triton/testing.py:150 do_bench -> synchronize), not in the forward proper. (2) SEPARATELY, my claim never acquired: JOB=$$ inside a script launched as `setsid bash script.sh &` is the LAUNCHER shells pid, which exits at once, so card_claim was handed a zombie 60 times -- "pid 994178 holds no GPU device fd: [bash] <defunct>" -- and the job ran UNCLAIMED for its whole life. Card 7 released and verified: 0 MiB, card_claim status "no claims, no orphans", held ~4 minutes.
- **Asked:** MEASURES ONE DESIGN NUMBER, not a result about the arms. Pairing tightens the SE of a between-arm loss difference by sqrt(2)/sqrt(2(1-corr)), so corr decides whether the paired-BPB estimator earns its complexity: at corr 0.95 pairing is worth 4.5x the eval set, at corr 0.5 it is worth 1.4x and is not. Prediction before the run: corr > 0.9, because the two arms differ only in head mixing and easy tokens are easy for both -- but the b0 arms val losses differ by 0.015-0.097 nat against a per-token SD of ~1 nat, which is consistent with high corr and is NOT a measurement of it. Reported with the DOCUMENT-CLUSTERED SE beside the naive one: tokens within a document are not independent, so the naive SE understates by sqrt(deff), and a design decision reads the clustered number. 64 val rows = 262144 tokens over ~64 documents.
- **Learned:** The claim refusal is correct and names the incident it exists for (b0_mem_m1 2026-09-04, a claim on a process not on a card while the card reads ORPHAN). My error was reading "claim with the job pid, not $$" as "use $$ inside the job script": under setsid the script shell is not the process holding the device, the python child is. The right fix is to claim the PYTHON pid ($! after launching it), or to have the probe claim from inside after torch.cuda.set_device -- the only point that can know a device is really held. On the crash: it is in autotune rather than my arithmetic, and the untested candidate is shape -- batch 4 x seq 4096 at world 1 is a per-rank shape the arms never ran (they trained batch 16 accum 2 world 2), so the autotuner was benchmarking configs for a shape/arch combination with no cache entry. Not established.
- **So:** Do not spend another RL-team card on a debug cycle. Card-free work first: fix the claim to use the python pid plus a selftest world that fails a $$-under-setsid claim by asserting the claimed pid is the one holding the device; then try to reproduce the misaligned address with a minimal forward (one checkpoint, batch 1, 1 row) to separate shape from model. Only then ask for one more window with a written prediction. Fallback that needs no card at all: size the paired-BPB design for corr=0.8 (~8000 docs for SE(Delta)=0.0039 nat) with the assumption stated rather than measured.

### e1_readout2_control_floor — NO NUMBERS -- the launch died 18s in, before scoring any item. RuntimeError "do not know how to restore data location of torch.storage.UntypedStorage (tagged with /work/aupai/data/tokenizer.json)" raised inside torch.load of the 892 MB checkpoint. Cause was mine and in the probe: main() called load_checkpoint(a.ckpt, a.tokenizer, a.device) against a signature of (path, device="cpu", dtype=None, fone_ok=True) returning (model, cfg), so the tokenizer path bound to device and reached torch.load as map_location. Fixed in main (content of the pre-rewrite a8379f08, now 7d5fe824) with a signature check as api_cloze selftest 12; both mutants verified RED on the intended assertion against a GREEN baseline in the same environment. Card 5 read 0 MiB immediately after, so no card was held past those 18s.
- **Asked:** THE CONTROL HALF ONLY, not a readout. ckpt_b0_headmix_armA read no memory table, so its seen-minus-unseen gap is the FLOOR that any arm gap must be measured against: whatever makes the seen region easier than the unseen one for a model that memorised nothing (different files, different API mix, region-level difficulty). Prereg memory_layers_0905 amendment_2 makes readout 2 the arm MINUS CONTROL difference of this gap, so this run produces two accuracies and a gap, and answers nothing about memorisation by itself. Recorded outcome: n per region, acc, naive SE and cluster SE with ICC and m_eff, and whether either region is within 1 SE of the chance floor -- in which case readout 2 is UNDEFINED at this scale on that region rather than null.
- **Learned:** The probe had ELEVEN green selftests while this bug sat in main(). Every one of them calls score_items or load_items with a stub model and a stub tokenizer, so not one entered main() at all -- the covered region was the scoring math and the launch path had zero coverage. Arity would not have caught it either: the bad call passed three arguments to a function that accepts four.
- **So:** Closed with no result rather than left running: nothing is on card 5, and a running row for a dead process is a ghost row. The relaunch is a NEW row, against the redacted item file (sha256 8524d9cb, 4998 items) rather than 8d61fab2 which the history rewrite removed, and only once the cards come back -- the RL team holds them by user order and 4c holds the lane.

### b0_mem_m2 — exit 1
- **Asked:** M2 at ONE pooled layer (512^2 = 262,144 values, 0.27B memory params, layer 6) against M1's 1,048,576 and M3's 1,428,025: readout 3's slope of block-paired doc_cu val against memory size, at equal dense params (206,128,200 all arms), with the fp32 table master
- **Learned:** monitor: process exited 1
- **So:** check the log

### b0_mem_m3 — stopped at step 652 by the controller under readout 4, alongside M1 (4c 2026-09-05). Last diag step 600: pool_touched_frac 0.0700 (stop below 0.20), topk_entropy 0.695, key_gini 0.9380 -- already past the threshold and on the same trajectory as M1, only steeper. Throughput ratio to the control was above the gate at every matched step: step 30 72937/82300 = 0.886, step 100 73165/82300 = 0.889 (4c predicted ~0.89). Readout 6 healthy: rows_changed_since_prev 1.09-1.25 at every window. Checkpoint: ckpt_b0_mem_m3.pt.interrupt.step652.
- **Asked:** M3 at ONE pooled layer (1195^2 = 1,428,025 values, 1.46B memory params, layer 6) against M1's 1,048,576 and the control: readout 3's slope of block-paired doc_cu val against memory size, at equal dense params (206,128,200 all three), with the fp32 table master
- **Learned:** Same key-usage collapse as M1 and faster at the larger table: 1,428,025 values reached 0.070 touched by step 600 where M1 at 1,048,576 was at 0.095 by step 1000. The larger the pool, the smaller the fraction the collapsed selection distribution covers, which is the direction a selection-side defect predicts and the opposite of what a value-update defect would give.
- **So:** Stopped before its own step 1000 because the fix is a design change that relaunches every arm for readout 3, so the remaining card time bought nothing. Not a null on readout 3: the size slope has no valid points at all.

### b0_mem_m3 — refused at launch: run_ddp.sh drift gate
- **Asked:** M3 at ONE pooled layer (1195^2 = 1,428,025 values, 1.46B memory params, layer 6) against M1's 1,048,576 and the control: readout 3's slope of block-paired doc_cu val against memory size, at equal dense params (206,128,200 all three), with the fp32 table master
- **Learned:** run_ddp.sh:34 REFUSED because pod_drift --check exits 1: pod AGENTS.md f6bd9d4f is BEHIND main 813a6568 by the section 201 that landed after the 22:32:20Z push. The pod was not edited -- the refusal message says it was, and that diagnosis is wrong for this cause. No card was touched, no rank started.
- **So:** 4c pushes main to the pod (scripts/pod_push.sh --all), then relaunch. NOT taking ALLOW_UNSYNCED=1: it would make M3 unreproducible for a docs-only difference.

### b0_mem_m1 — stopped at step 1065 by the controller under readout 4 (pre-registered pool collapse). Last diag step 1000: pool_touched_frac 0.0945 (stop below 0.20), topk_entropy 0.927 of max ln32=3.466, key_gini 0.9192. Throughput ratio to the control at the same step was healthy: step 30 73712/82300 = 0.896, step 100 73883/82300 = 0.898, above the 0.85 gate. Readout 6 healthy throughout: rows_changed_since_prev 1.09-1.20 of touched rows at every window, so the fp32 table master did its job and bf16 rounding is NOT the failure. Checkpoints: ckpt_b0_mem_m1.pt.step1000 and .interrupt.step1065.
- **Asked:** M1 at ONE pooled layer (1024^2, 1.07B memory params, layer 6) buys >=0.010 nat block-paired doc_cu val over ckpt_b0_headmix_armA at equal dense params (206,128,200 both sides), with the fp32 table master so updates do not round away in bf16
- **Learned:** The failure is key-usage collapse, not the value tables value updates. pool_touched_frac fell monotonically 0.306 (step 100) -> 0.214 -> 0.137 -> 0.113 -> 0.099 -> 0.095 -> 0.094 -> 0.094 -> 0.092 -> 0.0945 while key_gini rose 0.781 -> 0.919 and topk_entropy fell 2.37 -> 0.93. The selection distribution concentrates on a few percent of the pool; the rows it does select are updated correctly. ProductKeyMemory applies RMSNorm to the input and then Linear(d, 2*key_dim) straight into the two half-key top-k, with no normalisation of the query output -- Lample et al. 2019 3.3 BatchNorm the query network output and report it as needed to keep key usage spread.
- **So:** Readout 3 (the size slope) has no valid points: all three arms collapsed and M2/M3 were stopped with M1 under the same rule. Fix the query normalisation, verify on probes/mem_toy.py against the key-usage metric before any relaunch, then relaunch all three arms.

### b0_headmix_armB — doc_cu unweighted_mean 2.2824 nats/token (val, 9 domains, 576 blocks, 2,359,296 tokens) -- THE PRE-REGISTERED RULER. Train-path final val 2.200, not the ruler. humaneval gold BPB per-task mean 0.7176, domain_bpb 0.4491. Row ckpt_b0_headmix_armB.pt#cu (doc_cu, card 2 on fb's 11:45Z one-job loan), 9 metrics, in runs/score_matrix.jsonl. Params 208,552,008 non-embedding / 242,171,976 total against arm A's 206,128,200 / 239,748,168: +2,423,808 = +1.18% non-embedding, +1.01% whole-model, recounted from both checkpoints on disk.
- **Asked:** Pre-registered as b0_head_hybrid_3to1 in runs/prereg.jsonl (registered 08:29Z, amended 08:34Z, both BEFORE these runs started at 09:09/09:11Z). Arm B wins if doc_cu val BPB is lower by more than 0.003; loses if higher by more than 0.003; between is NO DIFFERENCE and the layer-level form stays. Arm B costs +1.18% parameters (206,128,200 -> 208,552,008) and what that buys at this scale is UNMEASURED, so an advantage smaller than that is undecidable. The design cannot separate attention count from per-layer width; a B win reads as 'this topology', not as either mechanism.
- **Learned:** Per-layer 6 KDA + 2 MLA heads (head_mixed=3, latent 256) loses to layer-level 3:1 at d1024 L12, 1B tokens, seed 42, on doc_cu by 0.087 nat, 576/576 blocks. The layer-level form stays. +1.18% non-embedding params in B runs against B, so the loss is not a parameter artifact; the design cannot separate attention count from per-layer width, so this is 'this topology loses', not a mechanism. Throughput: B 7.3% slower, +2.2 GiB at the clean step-30 read. One seed, one budget point. Measured: B-A = +0.087380 nat, SE 0.001077, t +81.12, 576/576 blocks worse, 9/9 domains, range +0.0642 (code_py_starcoder) to +0.1128 (chat_qa), one-sided p 4.04e-174 -- 29x the pre-registered 0.003 LOSE band, with the whole 95% interval (+0.0853, +0.0895) above it. Two independent per-byte rulers agree in sign: humaneval gold BPB +0.0347, domain_bpb +0.0179. The undecidability rule in amendment (1) does NOT apply: it guards against claiming an unattributable WIN, and B has none -- it is worse while carrying more parameters, so the confound works against the arm that lost.
- **So:** NOT ADOPTED. One asymmetry found in the checkpoints and not intended by the design, recorded so the next reader does not rediscover it: kv_down is 1024->256 in both arms but kv_up is (2048,256) in A and (512,256) in B, so both cache 256 numbers per token per layer while A reconstructs 2048 values from them and B reconstructs 512. That spends parameters rather than starving the path, so it does not explain the loss. Two metrics favour B and are reported rather than dropped: minimal_pairs 0.7509 -> 0.7906, mean margin 2.405 -> 2.723. This checkpoint stays KEEP until the user says otherwise (6e, 2026-09-04). GATE NOTE, and it is a real defect rather than a formality: check_score_matrix derives the checkpoint from the cmd and tests for '<ckpt>.pt' in runs/score_matrix.jsonl, but a doc_cu row is named '<ckpt>.pt#cu' by design (score_matrix.py:1602, so a doc_cu row cannot displace its cu_none pair). A run scored ONLY on doc_cu therefore reads as unscored. Arm A passed only because it happens to also carry a cu_none row under the bare name. Satisfied here with `reading_artifact`, which is the field 6e's 2026-09-04 ruling added for exactly this -- a row whose reading is a different quantity than the matrix's -- and the path must exist, so it cannot assert a reading nobody wrote. Reported to the harness owner rather than patched by me.

### e1_c11_doccu_rescore — four #cu domain_loss unweighted_mean, doc_cu path, card 5: ckpt_data_leg_206m_8b.pt#cu 1.8678, ckpt_params_leg_438m_3p76b.pt#cu 1.8668, ckpt_b0_sd_equalcompute.pt#cu 2.2126, ckpt_b0_n8_fixed.pt#cu 2.2545. All four in runs/score_matrix.jsonl with cu_path=doc_cu and the #cu name suffix, beside their cu_none rows.

Two summaries of one measurement disagree in direction, so neither is a direction. Per-domain doc_cu gain spans -0.0157 (code_py_rp1t) to -0.2364 (chatml), a 15x spread tracking documents-per-row -- so eff.eval_path_cu_artifact_ce's 'common-mode, cancels in a difference' holds to first order only: two checkpoints on one path cancel most of it, two different mixes do not.

### params_leg_final_score_matrix — CORRECTED 2026-09-04. l1_fewshot is PRESENT, not absent: 34/497 correct = acc 0.06841046, binomial_delta 0.06279858, answer_present_rate 0.45472837, demos 3, demo_lang zh, temperature 0.0, rep_stop True, arm ours, ckpt ckpt_params_leg_438m_3p76b.pt -- source runs/params_leg_438m_3p76b.log:1206, written by the chained score_matrix job's RETRY at 19:50 after eval_artifacts refused the 19:09 attempt. score_matrix.jsonl kept the FAILED attempt's error string instead of the successful retry, which is why the field reads as an error. Other metrics unchanged: domain_loss unweighted_mean 1.9335 across 11 domains (val); humaneval gold BPB 0.584477 per-task / 0.469392 byte-weighted over 164 tasks; lambada_en acc 0.2610 (SE 0.006118, 5153 items), nll_per_byte 0.764194; lambada_zh open_acc1 0.273 / acc5 0.496 / two-way 0.9259; minimal_pairs 0.7798 over 277 pairs, margin 2.8903 nats; math_v2_like 0.9797 over 3012 items; mc_ceval 26.6. domain_bpb REMAINS ABSENT -- exited 1 in both the streaming and the summary block with no retry, because the checkpoint has no vocab_id (old format); no backfill, a synthesised vocab_id would defeat the check. Six generative metrics skipped by profile.
- **Learned:** MY EARLIER ROW SAID l1_fewshot MUST BE READ AS ABSENT AND THAT WAS WRONG. The metric ran and scored; only the score_matrix.jsonl FIELD is missing it. I read a ledger field as the state of the thing it describes, twice within an hour -- the same shape as reading a captured 'exists (0 bytes)' error as a file's current size, when the file held 497 rows of real generations and the controller had ruled it deletable on my report. Both times the ledger was the older witness and the artifact was the newer one. The 497-row preds file carries only {q, gen, ok} with one uniform key set, so it cannot prove its own provenance; the filename, the summary's ckpt field, demos 3, and total 497 matching the row count are what tie it to this checkpoint.
- **So:** Row corrected: l1_fewshot 6.84% is citable from the log; domain_bpb absent. data/eval/preds_l1_d3_ckpt_params_leg_438m_3p76b.pt.zh.jsonl (402,174 B, 497 rows) NOT deleted, and its .REFUSED sidecar (396 B, 19:09:31) KEPT -- it is the only record that the guard fired and a retry followed, i.e. the evidence for this reconstruction. Two defects handed to de: score_matrix.jsonl keeps a failed attempt when a retry inside the same job succeeded, and preds rows carry no ckpt or demo count.

### ab_untie_head — ANSWERED WITHOUT A CARD, so the A/B is cancelled rather than queued.
- **Asked:** 1e 的问题：b0-10 的 embedding 放大是否来自 head 以 lr 0.1 训练。
- **Learned:** tied tok.weight already contains three row classes that each eat exactly one gradient path: [0,32773) input+head, [32773,32784) HEAD ONLY (in head.weight[:vocab], never an input id, never a target), [32784,32832) neither. step2500->3000: head-only rows 13.2399 -> 13.8540 = 1.0464x, input+head median 76.6132 -> 90.2697 = 1.1783x, no-path rows max abs delta exactly 0 (so the read has no noise floor). The head path DOES amplify -- 1e's direction is right -- but it is ln1.0464/ln1.1783 = 27.6% of the growth; 72.4% is the input path. The 11 head-only rows are bit-identical to each other, so that path's gradient is a term SHARED by all rows (the softmax denominator), which is also the mechanism behind 4.6's 'same multiple at every quantile'.
- **So:** Do not run it. Untie is also NOT parameter-matched: our head is tied (model.py:322), so untying adds 33.6M params = +17% at 200M, moving capacity and lr together -- an honest test needs three arms (tied / untied-same-lr / untied-low-lr), 3x the cost, to answer a question the free read already answered. If anyone runs it anyway, the reading is 'do head-only rows stop growing', NOT total norm, which will only fall by under a third and read as 'no effect'.

### b0_n8_fixed — val 2.172 (100-batch fixed prefix), train 1.816, 3815 steps = 1.0001B tokens, 4399s, 116K tok/s/gpu steady. Paired against ckpt_b0_sd_unlooped.pt on doc_cu: corpus loss -0.024353 nat (sd 0.015983, SE 0.000666, t -36.57, 552/576 blocks down, 9/9 domains); humaneval gold BPB +0.014674 (SE 0.004612, t +3.18, 97/164 tasks WORSE).
- **Learned:** THE TWO RULERS DISAGREE IN SIGN. Corpus loss clears the pre-registered -0.005 threshold 5x; humaneval is significantly the WRONG WAY at t +3.18. So the registered first clause is NOT met and the fix does not pass on evidence. The leak was costing accuracy is NOT the finding -- that was a val-based guess and humaneval refutes it as a general claim. AND THE DOSE-RESPONSE IS ABSENT: benefit correlates only -0.560 with documents per row against -0.951 for the leak itself, and zh_web at 2 eos/row gains as much as chatml at 18, so the mechanism behind the gain is not established by this A/B.
- **So:** Fix enters the recipe FOR CORRECTNESS with the disagreement recorded (6e second clause), not on evidence. Recorded as eff.conv_doc_isolation_ab_200m. The missing dose-response goes to e1 as an open question: row-start effect (the masking also zeroes taps at the first 3 positions of every ROW) versus a general training-dynamics perturbation. One arm trained, not two: the current arm is ckpt_b0_sd_unlooped.pt reused after scripts/b0_n8_reuse_gate.py verified 576/576 blocks bitwise with the flag OFF and cu PASSED.

### b0_sd_equalcompute — val 2.135 (100-batch fixed prefix), train 2.221, 4824 steps = 1.2646B tokens, 5517s, 116K tok/s/gpu steady. Paired against ckpt_b0_sd_looped.pt on doc_cu: corpus loss -0.043905 nat (sd 0.032431, SE 0.001351, t -32.49, 544/576 blocks down); humaneval gold BPB -0.038320 (SE 0.005945, t -6.45, 123/164 better). Against the unlooped arm: -0.066231 nat (t -44.05) and -0.031561 BPB (t -6.64).
- **Learned:** THE LOOP LOSES PER FLOP. Three rulers agree in sign for the first time: the same compute spent on 26 percent more tokens beats the looped arm on corpus likelihood, code likelihood and val. 26 percent more tokens is worth 0.066 nat and the loop bought 0.022 of that for the same FLOPs, so it captures about a third of what the plain token spend captures. The loop wins per token and loses per FLOP; neither half is usable alone.
- **So:** N7 middle-layer loop NOT ADOPTED. Recorded as repo.loop_not_adopted_equal_compute. The 4824-step count came from the measured 6N ratio 1.264604, not 16/12 = 1.3333, which the arms own throughput and wall clock exclude; 5085 steps would have handed this arm 5.4 percent more compute than the looped arm had.

### n7c_p3_prefix3 — HumanEval gold BPB, 164 tasks / 29662 bytes asserted per cell. twin causal 0.4643 | arm+prefix 0.4871 (delta +0.0228, paired SE 0.0017, z -13.7, 151/164 items WORSE) | arm+causal 0.4612 (delta -0.0031, SE 0.0006, z +5.5, 38/164 worse). Training loss tracked the twin exactly: step110 1.309 vs 1.311, step210 1.301 vs 1.300, final 1.058 vs 1.057, at 19s/10 steps for both.
- **Asked:** Prefix-LM on all three MLA layers (3,7,11) of the UNLOOPED model beats its causal twin ckpt_n7c_unlooped.pt on HumanEval gold BPB. Twin is an existing 500-step run at the same pack/seed/steps, so no baseline is retrained. READING RULE, pre-registered: adopt only if the paired delta is negative (better) by more than 2x its paired SE on the 164-task intersection AND the per-item count favours prefix; a delta inside 2 SE is a null. Scored in its own topology (unlooped) plus the causal-scored mismatch cell. SCOPE: the intervention is training-side only -- eval applies the same prefix mask at scoring time on 164/164 tasks. Gate-certified on card 4 (pod ab92954d): A/A2/B/B2/C/D/E/F all pass for layer set [3,7,11]; leak decided on the mask by exact enumeration (292919 newly visible pairs, 0 with a supervised key); F confirms all 12564 supervised positions move, max|dlogit| 14.67.
- **Learned:** THE PROVISIONAL FLAG IS LIFTED AND +0.0228 STANDS. The eval-side wiring is cleared by scripts/n7c_path_agree.py on card 4 (pod, 2026-09-03 22:0xZ), and the two earlier readings of that script are both retracted: (1) its FIRST run printed max|dlogit| 4.6910 and I read it as 'the eval path is broken'. It had no control row. With one added the CAUSAL control disagreed by 3.9619 with no mask anywhere, so packing six tasks into one row moves the logits 3-4 on its own -- more than the effect under test -- and that run could never have isolated the mask. model.py:122-131 does pass cu_seqlens into chunk_kda, so the boundaries reach the kernel; that is not sufficient for a packed row to equal an unpacked one here. (2) the SHAPE-MATCHED rerun (one task, one row, one document, cu/aux hand-built as humaneval_bpb.py does versus doc_cu_seqlens/doc_prompt_lengths as sft_math.py does) agreed at max|dlogit| 0.4833 prefix / 0.3106 causal with aux ALL MATCH -- but 0.5 was MY threshold and a max logit difference is not a bound on BPB. THE NUMBER THAT SETTLES IT is the same quantity the row is written in: gold BPB computed from both constructions on those six tasks reads 0.3800 eval versus 0.3804 training, a difference of +0.0004 over 1119 solution bytes. That is 56x smaller than the +0.0228 under test, so the construction cannot account for the delta. ALSO REFUTED ON THE WAY, the leading hypothesis for a wiring bug: doc_prompt_lengths returned [117, 131, 83, 118, 123, 92] and the eval site's len(prompt) for the same tasks is identical, so the two sites do not derive different prompt lengths. SO THE ORIGINAL FINDING IS RESTORED: the matched cell fails the pre-registered rule in the wrong direction (+0.0228, 13.7 SE, 151/164 items worse, the count agreeing so it is not a few large items), while the SAME weights scored causally read 0.4612, better than the twin by 0.0031 at z +5.5 with 38/164 worse. Prefix training did not damage the weights; applying the bidirectional prompt mask at inference costs 0.0228, about 7.4x the weight-time gain. WHAT IS STILL NOT ESTABLISHED, and it is not small: eval scores ONE document per row and training packs ~250, and the invalid run measured that packing alone moves these logits by 3-4 regardless of mask. So this arm's numbers are internally consistent -- every cell scored one-per-row -- but the regime the model TRAINED in was never scored, and 6e's original suspicion that a shape difference sits between the two sites is correct in substance even though it does not explain the 0.0228. The prompt-length binning refutation stands: the penalty is flat across quartiles (+0.0350 / +0.0287 / +0.0346 / +0.0296, 37-39 of 41 worse in every bin).
- **So:** NOT ADOPTED, as originally ruled, now on a verified number. The weights-only gain (-0.0031, z +5.5) is real but small, is collected by training with prefix and scoring causally, and is NOT recommended on one 500-step run at n=1 seed with seed sigma unmeasured -- 0.0031 is inside the range a seed could move. Recorded as a directional finding, not a recipe. Durable outputs: eff.flash_attn_cute_mask_mod_backward_wrong_sm90 and its two-call workaround; and the packing measurement above, which says a single-document eval is a different regime from a packed training batch by 3-4 logits and is worth its own fact if anything else in this repo compares the two.

### n7c_p7_prefix7 — HumanEval gold BPB, 164 tasks / 29662 bytes asserted per cell, all cells scored looped 4-7 as the arm trained. twin causal 0.4676 | arm+prefix 0.4673 (delta -0.0003, paired SE 0.0003, z +1.1, 73/164 items worse) | arm+causal 0.4666 (delta -0.0010, SE 0.0002, z +4.0, 67/164 worse). Final training loss 1.057, identical to the twin's 1.057; 19s/10 steps for both.
- **Asked:** Prefix-LM on MLA layer 7 ALONE, inside the looped block, beats its causal twin ckpt_n7c_looped.pt on HumanEval gold BPB. This is the closest well-defined form of the original one-layer proposal. THE LOOP IS HELD CONSTANT: both this arm and its twin are looped 4-7, so prefix and loop are not confounded -- only the mask differs. Twin is an existing 500-step run at the same pack/seed/steps. READING RULE, pre-registered: adopt only if the paired delta is negative (better) by more than 2x its paired SE on the 164-task intersection AND the per-item count favours prefix; a delta inside 2 SE is a null. Scored in its own topology (looped 4-7) plus the causal-scored mismatch cell. Gate-certified on card 4 (pod ab92954d): A/A2/B/B2/C/D/E/F all pass for layer set [7]; the gate's real-length loss read -2.16e-04 vs causal, which is the effect under test and NOT a leak -- leakage is decided on the mask by exact enumeration (292919 newly visible pairs, 0 with a supervised key, 0 with a query outside the prompt).
- **Learned:** THE PROVISIONAL FLAG IS LIFTED; the null stands. The eval path both arms were scored through is cleared by the same measurement that cleared P3's: with the shape held fixed the eval and training constructions produce gold BPB within +0.0004 of each other (0.3800 vs 0.3804 over 1119 solution bytes), 56x below the effect P3 turned on and far below anything that could move a -0.0003 delta. The arm reads -0.0003 against SE 0.0003, inside 2 SE, with 73/164 items worse, indistinguishable from chance. The training curve tracked its causal twin exactly (1.057 vs 1.057). Same scope caveat as P3: every cell was scored one document per row, and the packed regime the model trained in was not scored.
- **So:** NOT ADOPTED. Prefix-LM at MLA layer 7 alone under the 4-7 loop is a null on HumanEval gold BPB at 500 steps, n=1 seed. No re-score needed: the construction check that cleared P3 covers this arm's eval path too.

### params_leg_final_score_matrix — score_matrix row landed in runs/score_matrix.jsonl, measured 2026-09-03, type base, profile full, ckpt_params_leg_438m_3p76b.pt (438.36M). domain_loss unweighted_mean 1.9335 (val, 152.5s); humaneval gold BPB 0.584477 per-task mean / 0.469392 byte-weighted over 164 tasks; lambada_en acc 0.2610 (SE 0.006118, 5153 items) and nll_per_byte 0.764194; lambada_zh open_acc1 0.273 / acc5 0.496 / two-way 0.9259; minimal_pairs 0.7798 over 277 pairs, mean margin 2.8903 nats; math_v2_like 0.9797 over 3012 items; mc_ceval 26.6. Six generative metrics SKIPPED by profile (math_hard, code_500_v2, code_500, math_500, mc_full, pass_at_k). TWO METRICS ERRORED and are recorded as errors rather than as values: domain_bpb exited 1 (checkpoint has no vocab_id, old format, so the tokenizer cross-check could not run), and l1_fewshot exited 1 (eval_artifacts refused to overwrite an existing 0-byte preds_l1_d3_..zh.jsonl; needs --force or a cleanup, and the refusal is correct -- an eval artifact is the only copy of what a checkpoint generated).
- **Asked:** the params leg's final score, with per-block output, so b0-23 can pair it against the data leg on blocks (n=64/domain); expect 45-50 min from the three step-N precedents
- **Learned:** The row exists and the run finished; the row stayed status=running because run_ddp.sh chains score_matrix after training and nothing closes the exp row when that chained job exits. Not a measurement finding -- a ledger one. Two of the eleven metrics carry errors, so this row is not a complete readout of the params leg: domain_bpb is unavailable for this checkpoint at all (no vocab_id in an old-format checkpoint) and l1_fewshot needs the stale 0-byte artifact resolved before it can produce a number.
- **So:** Row closed as ok, with the two metric errors named in the result rather than smoothed. Anyone citing the params leg's domain_bpb or l1_fewshot must read them as ABSENT, not as zero. Closing this also clears no_ghost_running on the pod.

### e1_31b_loop_500 — humaneval BPB 2x2, 164 tasks / 29662 bytes: UtSu 0.4643, LtSl 0.4676, UtSl 0.4883, LtSu 0.4852. Diagonal: looped-train/looped-score is 0.0033 BPB WORSE than unlooped/unlooped (SE 0.0006, |z| 5.7, 112/164 items worse). Mismatch cells are 0.0240 (|z| 11.0) and 0.0209 (|z| 9.0) worse -- 6-7x the diagonal
- **Asked:** N7 STAGE B RERUN AT 500 STEPS PER ARM (user order via 6e, 2026-09-04). At 250 steps the diagonal -- each arm scored in its own topology -- was +0.0023 BPB (SE 0.0006, z +3.6, 103/164 tasks worse) with training loss identical to 0.001 (1.334 vs 1.335): the looped arm trained equally well and generalised worse. QUESTION: is that gap closing at 500 steps or is it the asymptote? PRE-REGISTERED READING: the DIAGONAL decides. A 500-step diagonal below +0.0023 = closing; at or above = the cost is not a warmup artifact. The two MISMATCH cells are reported beside it, not as the result: at 250 steps they were +0.0193 and +0.0264, 8-11x the diagonal and in OPPOSITE directions, which is why Stage A's +0.0273 was a topology mismatch rather than the loop. BOTH ARMS ARE FRESH 500, NOT RESUMED, and the reason is a defect not a choice: the 250-step checkpoints carry ['cfg','corpus_fp','env_fp','vocab_id'] and the .step200 files add 'step' -- no optimizer state, because sft_math.py's mid-run save computed the snapshot on the line above and did not pass it (fixed 148e6027, guarded scripts/test_sft_save_opt.py). Resuming from that restarts Adam moments and the LR schedule, which is a new run wearing the word resume. CONSEQUENCE: the existing 250 point is an INDEPENDENT run at a different step count, not an earlier snapshot of this one, so the side-by-side compares two runs and not one curve. --save_every 250 makes THIS run land a mid-run checkpoint WITH optimizer state so the next extension resumes for real.
- **Learned:** the topology mismatch dominates: any single-topology reading of this experiment measures the mismatch, not looping. Looping is worse on the diagonal at 500 steps, and the 250-step diagonal was 0.0023 worse in the same direction -- two separate runs, so that pair is a direction, not a slope
- **So:** loop 4 7 does not recover code BPB at 500 steps; N7c closed. Any later loop reading scores under the topology it trained with and reports the mismatch cells beside the diagonal

### e1_31_middle_layer_loop — NOT ADOPTED. 2x2 humaneval BPB byte-weighted (164 tasks / 29,662 bytes every cell): U-trained/U-scored 0.4635, U/L 0.4899, L/U 0.4828, L/L 0.4658. domain_loss unweighted_mean, head_fp identical on all 9 domains: 1.9858 / 2.1177 / 2.0810 / 1.9951. DIAGONAL (each arm in the topology it was trained in) U-U -> L-L: +0.0023 bits/byte, paired SE 0.0006, z +3.6, 103/164 tasks worse; +0.0093 nat, 9/9 domains worse, per-domain range +0.0054 to +0.0191. ms/token seq 1024 batch 1, 60 iters: looped/unlooped 1.336/1.284/1.329 on the SFT ckpt (mean 1.317, sd 0.028) and 1.223 on the data-leg ckpt, all four in [1.223, 1.336]. Training loss 250/250 identical to 0.001 (1.334 vs 1.335). unlooped path reproduces model._body BITWISE (max|diff| 0.000e+00) on every run
- **Asked:** N7: does running middle blocks twice buy depth at inference? RULED SEMANTICS (6e, option 3, pre-registered before any number): under AttnRes the second visit ADDS INTO the source the first visit wrote, so the ledger length is unchanged and final_ar still sees 25. Two premises of the ruling's wording are false on this checkpoint and the wrapper implements the semantics, not the wording -- attn_res_blocks=0 makes ar_block_ends {1..24} so 'partial' is always [] at the ar() read and the live accumulator is the done entry; and AttnRes has no per-source weight (g:(d,), q:(d,) only), so option 1 is the wrong experiment because it changes the depth-attention DISTRIBUTION, not because a weight vector runs out. KDA state rule (a) is the DEFINITION on this path, never '(a) beat (b)': model.py:125 passes no initial_state and discards the returned state, and the scoring path is one teacher-forced forward with no KV slots, so each visit starts from zero state by absence of plumbing. READING: humaneval gold BPB byte-weighted with paired per-task SE, domain_loss unweighted_mean with head_fp asserted equal, ms/token. Each arm must be scored in the topology it was TRAINED in; scoring loop-trained weights unlooped measures a weights-topology mismatch, not the loop.
- **Learned:** THE MISMATCH IS THE FINDING, NOT THE LOOP. The two off-diagonal cells are 8-11x the diagonal and point in OPPOSITE directions: loop-trained weights run without the loop +0.0193 (z +8.4), normal weights run with the loop +0.0264 (z +11.3). So the cost at this scale is that weights and topology must MATCH, and either mismatch costs an order of magnitude more than the loop itself. This retro-explains Stage A: its +0.0273 is the mismatch-B cell (+0.0264), so Stage A measured the mismatch and never measured the loop -- and my first Stage B report repeated the error by scoring both arms unlooped, which controls the evaluation while breaking the variable that matters (6e caught it; 88% of the penalty I reported was my own design). Fairly scored the loop is slightly WORSE on both rulers and costs ~1.3x forward compute. Training loss being identical while both held-out rulers move against it means this is not 'the looped arm trained worse' but 'trained equally well, generalizes worse'; the 20-step probe's 1.375-vs-1.471 gap closed completely by step 250 and must not be quoted as an effect
- **So:** Middle-layer looping is not adopted. Two numbers I published are RETRACTED: the 1.64x latency (one 20-iter run; the same checkpoint at 60 iters reads 1.223 and 1.640 is outside the range of all four reps, so ~1.3x is the figure) and 'training under the loop recovers 28% of the penalty' (there was no +0.0193 penalty to recover). BOUNDARY: 250 SFT steps on a 206M checkpoint rules out CHEAP adoption, not SMELT's from-scratch-under-the-loop claim, which we did not run. A third reading nobody ran: whether a model pretrained under the loop behaves differently. Artifacts committed under runs/n7_2x2/ -- four summaries carrying explicit cell/trained/scored fields plus four 164-row per-task preds files, verified to reproduce every published figure including SE 0.0006 and 103/164 from the repo alone with no pod access

### ab_untie_head — THREE ARMS, TWO DIFFERENCES, and both differences are 9/9 same-sign but under the 0.24 nat bar. domain_loss unweighted_mean: base 2.8480 -> arm2 (untied@embed 0.1, +33,619,968 params = +16.3%) 2.8962 -> arm3 (untied@own 0.003464, IDENTICAL param count to arm2) 2.9139. base->arm2 +0.0482 nat (20% of the bar), 9/9 domains worse, sd 0.0342, t +4.23. arm2->arm3 +0.0176 (7% of the bar), 9/9 worse, sd 0.0116, t +4.55. base->arm3 +0.0659 (27%), 9/9, t +5.38. Per-domain a2-base spans +0.0078 (en_c4) to +0.0977 (zh_web), 12.5x; relative spans +0.221% to +3.403%, 15.4x, and the two orderings DISAGREE (zh_web is 1st absolute, 4th relative), so the domain structure is NOT a shadow of loss magnitude -- corr(base loss, absolute delta) = +0.592. minimal_pairs NOT READABLE by construction: the five 500-step A/B checkpoints all sit inside 4.33pt (sd 1.67pt) against an 11.5pt bar, so this metric cannot resolve any arm at this budget. val loss 2.603 -> 2.647 -> 2.668; train 2.332 -> 2.378 -> 2.383.
- **Asked:** b0-17: is the LM head's lr the mechanism behind the uniform embedding growth? THREE ARMS, TWO DIFFERENCES, because one difference cannot separate the two candidate explanations -- this is the confound A/B (4) could not close and 1e's ruling fixes it here (2026-09-03):
  arm 1  base (REUSED ckpt_ab_shapelr_base.pt)  tied head, trains at embed_lr 0.1
  arm 2  --untie_head                            own head weights, STILL at embed_lr 0.1
  arm 3  --untie_head --head_lr 0.003464         own weights AND its own AdamW group
base->arm2 isolates CAPACITY (+33,619,968 params = +16.3%, 206,128,200 -> 239,748,168; the SAME magnitude as A/B (4)'s value_embed table, because both add one padded_vocab x d tensor). arm2->arm3 isolates the LR MECHANISM at IDENTICAL parameter count. WHY THE LR IS THE SUSPECT: train.py routes any parameter whose name contains tok or head into the embed group, so today's tied head necessarily trains at 0.1 -- 28.9x the nanochat reference head lr 0.004*(d/768)^-0.5 = 0.003464 at d1024 -- and b0-10 measured tok.weight growing 1.43x per 500 steps with 7.9% effective same-direction rate.
READING (pre-registered): domain_loss unweighted mean against 0.24 nat (ds.seed_variance_0p2b), minimal_pairs against 11.5pt (be.minimal_pairs_seed_variance), and tok.weight growth per 500 steps against b0-10's 1.43x. Verdict written as TWO differences, never one: a flat base->arm2 with a moving arm2->arm3 means the lr matters and the capacity does not; both flat means neither is readable at this scale and the +16.3% is unpaid-for; base->arm2 moving alone means this is a capacity result wearing an lr costume.
BASE IS REUSED, matched on the four things that decide comparability: world 4, 500 steps, mix_200m_4b, seed 42. Its cfg carries NO untie_head/head_lr field, which is the default (False, 0.0) -- absence here is not drift. Tokens/step therefore match, unlike the (3)/(2a) pair where world 5 vs world 4 made the same step a different token position and inverted the sign.
GUARD: scripts/test_untie_head.py, four checks each verified red on its own broken world. The one that matters most is that arms 2 and 3 are indistinguishable in every log except one lr field, so arm 3 collapsing into arm 2 errors nowhere and prints one number for two experiments; head lr is read by TENSOR IDENTITY, not group name, because a missing group returns None and would pass as a match. Also pinned: untying zeroes tok's vocab pad rows (while tied, one zero_() covered both tensors, so untied they would train in one arm only -- 1e caught this before the run), and the head stays fp8-excluded by name.
- **Learned:** THE HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED, NOT UNSUPPORTED, AND THE REFUTATION IS IN THE WEIGHTS NOT THE LOSS. 1e's mechanism was that the TIED head trains tok.weight at embed_lr 0.1 through a second gradient path, which would make tok's uniform growth (b0-10) an artifact of that path. Removing the path must then reduce the growth. It INCREASES it: tok row-norm median over the input+head class [0,32773), which is what runs/b0_17_rows.json stores: 16.9211 -> 18.3390 (arm2, x1.083796) -> 19.8118 (arm3, x1.170835). The WHOLE-TABLE median over all 32832 rows is a different quantity and reads 16.9115 -> 18.3308 -> 19.8036, lower because it includes the 48 no-path rows near 0.62 and the 11 pad rows; an earlier version of this row quoted the whole-table numbers with the class-based ratios, and the ratios themselves were 4-decimal medians divided rather than the actual ratio (e1's independent recompute caught both). The arm with the LOWER head lr had the LARGER tok growth -- one comparison across two untied points, which is not a trend and not monotone (two points are always ordered), and the lower-lr arm's head is undertrained at 2.4x init, which is a candidate explanation for that ordering. A third head_lr would be needed before direction is a claim. The 11 alignment-padding rows [32773,32784) settle it, because they are never an input id and never a target, so they are reachable ONLY through the head and their movement IS that path rather than a proxy: arm2 and arm3 both read EXACTLY 0.0000 there (path completely removed, not weakened), while arm2's HEAD pad rows read 7.1036 against the tied base's 6.6575 (the path itself got 1.067x STRONGER). Named path fully removed, not weakened, effect moved the other way. WHAT REPLACES IT IS A GUESS, labelled: a tied table receives input-path and head-path gradients that partially cancel, and untying removes the cancellation so the input path alone pushes tok further; no gradient signs were measured, so only 'the head path inflates tok' is excluded. ARM 3 DOES NOT MEASURE THE LR MECHANISM AT STEADY STATE: its head reaches row-norm median 1.5375 = 2.4x the 0.64 init, i.e. barely trained, because 500 steps at 28.9x lower lr is not enough. Arm 2's head DID converge -- 16.4952 against the tied table's 16.9115, within 2.4% -- so arm 2's +0.0482 is not an initialization debt, while arm 3's extra +0.0176 largely is. CAPACITY IS NOT FREE AND NOT PAID FOR: +16.3% params makes every one of nine domains worse.
- **So:** NOT ADOPTED, and untie_head stays default False. Three consequences. (1) b0-10's uniform embedding growth needs a different mechanism: the head-path explanation is excluded by measurement, and the cancellation guess is the next thing to test -- it needs gradient signs on the two paths, not another A/B. (2) A/B (4) and b0-17 agree ONLY on the negative, and I overstated this before checking it: both spend +33,619,968 params (+16.3%) on one padded_vocab x d table and NEITHER pays for itself, but they are NOT the same result. value_embed is genuine noise -- per-domain delta 3/9 positive, mean -0.0002 nat, t -0.05, i.e. indistinguishable from zero in both sign and magnitude. The untied head is CONSISTENTLY WORSE -- 9/9 positive, mean +0.0482, t +4.23. So the shared claim is narrow: at 200M/500 steps neither use of that parameter budget helps. Calling them 'two independent arms of the same shape' would have claimed a reproduced effect where one arm has no effect at all, and the aggregate 'flat-to-worse' phrasing hides exactly that difference. (3) The copy-init arm (untied head initialized FROM the embedding table) is DEFERRED, not dropped -- 1e's ruling, and the distinction is the point. Its purpose did change: not to control for the initialization confound (arm 2's head converged to within 2.4% of the tied table, so that confound is bounded) but as the only arm that could separate tok's reverse growth from initialization cost. But it answers a MECHANISM question, it has no measured loss consequence to chase, and nothing about the recipe changes at this ladder point whatever it finds. Deferred says the question stays live with no card claimed; dropped would say the question is closed, and it is not. minimal_pairs should be dropped from 500-step A/B readings at 200M: 4.33pt of spread against an 11.5pt bar is not a measurement.

### ab_value_embed — FLAT on both pre-registered readings. domain_loss unweighted_mean base 2.8480 -> VE 2.8479, delta -0.0001 nat against a 0.24 nat bar (0.04% of it); minimal_pairs base 0.7148 -> VE 0.7040, -1.08pt against an 11.5pt bar (9.4% of it, and in the WRONG direction). val loss 2.603 -> 2.598 (-0.005, 2% of the bar), train 2.332 -> 2.324. Per-domain the sign is split 6 of 9 to VE and every domain moves less than 0.02: math_owm -0.0060, cot -0.0050, textbook -0.0061, zh_web -0.0016, code_starcoder -0.0112, code_rp1t -0.0064 for VE; en_c4 +0.0037, chat_qa +0.0112, chatml +0.0200 against. Both arms world 4, same 500 steps, 0.26B tokens, seed 42, mix_200m_4b, and the arm's cfg-diff line reads exactly: `cfg non-default: accum=2, anneal_frac=0.0, batch=16, mix='data/mix_200m_4b.json', value_embed=True, warmdown=0.1, warmup=300` -- value_embed is the only mechanism field, so arm identity is in the log rather than inferred from the checkpoint. Base is ckpt_ab_shapelr_base.pt.ep1, REUSED from A/B (2a); unlike the (3)/(2a) pair it is world 4 like this arm, so tokens/step match and the same step is the same token position. Two differences are by design and neither is a defect: the parameter difference (+33,659,904, +16.3%) IS the intervention, and the tree difference between the two runs is outside the training path. Throughput: both arms hold 57K tok/s/gpu at steady state, so the +16.3% parameters cost no measured tokens/s here -- stated as tok/s and param count only, NOT as free compute: reported MFU rises 48% -> 55% purely because mfu = 6*n_params*tps/peak (train.py:2560) puts n_params in the numerator while 99.9% of the added params are a gather with no matmul. Wall 1321s -> 1250s.
- **Learned:** THE TABLE IS FULLY TRAINED, WHICH KILLS THE PRE-REGISTERED WATCH RATHER THAN ANSWERING IT. The row-norm reading (scripts/ve_row_norms.py, runs/ve_row_norms.json) reads 32832 of 32832 rows above the decay floor -- not a few frequent rows. The WATCH text assumed 'a small fraction' of rows get touched at 500 steps; that was wrong by construction: 0.26B tokens over 32832 rows is ~7900 hits per row on average. So the 'few frequent rows won' branch was never live, and with the A/B flat there is no win to attribute either way. Movement is also NOT concentrated: the top 1000 rows of 32832 hold 15.9% of total delta, top 100 hold 2.7%, top 10 hold 0.3%.
THE FLOOR HAD TO BE MEASURED, AND THE ESTIMATED ONE INVERTED THE ANSWER. Three premises died before this number was usable, all of them caught: (a) 'rows differing from init were trained' reads the whole vocab, because the table sits in the AdamW `embed` group (train.py:779) and an Embedding's gradient is DENSE, so decoupled decay steps every row whether a token reached it or not; (b) 'a norm cutoff separates them' fails too -- the model inits embeddings to std=0.02 (model.py:453 via self.apply(self._init) at :388), so an untouched row keeps norm 0.02*sqrt(1024) = 0.64, and I had earlier quoted 32 from torch's N(0,1) default, which is not this model's init; (c) the floor I first used was ESTIMATED at 10x the median row on the premise that most rows stay untouched, and that premise is the very thing being tested -- it put the floor ~900x too high and reported 274 of 32832 rows trained. The floor is now MEASURED: step the real optimizer through the real schedule on a row whose gradient is always zero, giving 0.0322 relative (analytic 0.0323 for embed_lr=0.1 at train.py:263 with embed_wd=0.001 at :265 -- note line 820's decay-to-zero is inside `if isinstance(opt, Muon)` and never reaches this group). Every row clears it by >=2.65x, so the verdict does not sit near the 1.5x admission line. The selftest now carries the fully-trained table as a regression and asserts the 10x-median floor still fails it.
THE GATE-AT-1.5 INIT WORKS, which is what makes the flat result interpretable rather than a null launch: the arms track each other from the first logged step (step 10: 6.801 vs 6.798; step 100: 4.121 vs 4.110), so the VE arm is not spending its budget discovering a table hidden behind a zero gate.
- **So:** NOT ADOPTED. +16.3% parameters for -0.0001 nat is the whole case against it, and no seeds will be spent widening the bar for a change that is not being adopted -- the same call as A/B (3). Asymmetric summary, on purpose: there is NO evidence VE helps at 200M/0.26B, and the one non-flat reading (minimal_pairs -1.08pt) points the wrong way while staying inside its own bar, so 'harmless' is also unproven. WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT SETTLE: the hypothesis was never separated from capacity -- no equal-parameter control arm ran -- so a flat result cannot distinguish 'the mechanism does nothing' from 'the mechanism helps and the +16.3% of parameters spent on a gather hurts by the same amount'. It also does not transfer up: value embeddings are a nanogpt-speedrun result at a different scale and token budget, and a table fully trained at 0.26B may behave differently once the vocab is seen far more or far less often. To revisit, the cheap next step is the equal-parameter control (spend the 33.7M on width or depth instead), not another VE seed.

### control_lr_scan — killed mid-run (deletion window 2026-09-03)
- **Asked:** At seq 2048 (Pythia-160m max_position_embeddings, 2.18% of examples dropped vs 12.00% at 1024), the control arm has a held-out optimum inside {3e-5,1e-4,3e-4}; selection on held-out loss picks a different point than final training loss would, and the best point is not at a range endpoint.

### ab_zeroinit — PREDICTION REFUTED IN DIRECTION, FLAT IN MAGNITUDE. Every reading is worse for the arm and not one reaches its bar. domain_loss unweighted mean 2.7872 -> 2.9068, +0.1196 nat (0.50x the 0.24 bar, 2.32 sigma at sigma=0.0516); worse in 9 of 9 domains, per-domain +0.0601 (zh_web) .. +0.1750 (chatml), sd 0.0316, largest single domain 0.73x bar. minimal_pairs overall 0.7473 -> 0.7112, -3.61 pt (0.31x the 11.5 pt bar, 1.46 sigma at sd=2.47); mean_margin_nats 1.8421 -> 1.4781 (-0.3640; a margin, no bar registered for it). Per dimension: classifier +0.00 (n=42), numeric -2.00 (n=100), word_order -3.03 (n=99), factual -12.50 (n=16), function_word -15.00 (n=20) -- the two largest sit where one flipped pair is 6.2 and 5.0 pt, i.e. inside single-pair noise. Train loss (smoke only): last-10 mean 2.4174 -> 2.5620 (+0.1446), last-1 2.323 -> 2.438 (+0.1150), step-500 val 2.569 -> 2.682 (+0.1130), ep1 val 2.552 -> 2.663 (+0.1110). The early-trajectory half of the prediction also failed: at step 100 the arm was ahead (4.405 vs 4.451) but behind by step 300 (2.713 vs 2.575) and stayed behind -- the gentler start did not survive, which is the opposite of 'the advantage shows early'.
- **Asked:** A/B (3) speedrun record: zero-init every output projection (.o in both KDA and MLA blocks, FFN .w2; NOT AttnRes final_ar) so each sublayer starts as an identity on the residual stream. Two arms, 500 steps, equal tokens/seed/data, IDENTICAL parameter count -- so the delta needs no capacity caveat. PREDICTION: the arm is no worse, and its advantage if any shows in the first ~100 steps rather than at 500, because what zero-init buys is a gentler early trajectory. READING: domain_loss (readable move 0.24 nat, ds.seed_variance_0p2b) + minimal_pairs (11.5pt, be.minimal_pairs_seed_variance). Train loss is a smoke check only. A delta below threshold is NOT a result in either direction. KNOWN AND NOT A BUG: w13/qkv gradients are EXACTLY zero on step 0 in the zero arm (sublayer output is 0*x) and recover at step 1 -- scripts/test_zero_init_out.py pins both halves; still-zero after step 1 would be a real dead branch.
- **Learned:** FLAT, NOT FLOOR, and the two words carry different follow-ups. The instrument resolves 0.24 nat and this effect is 0.12 -- so it is readable-but-under-threshold (flat), not unjudgeable (floor). Per the pre-registered rule a sub-threshold delta is not a result in either direction, and that rule binds here: this experiment does NOT establish that zero-init costs 0.12 nat. What it does establish is the SIGN. All 13 readings (9 domains + 4 loss/val) point the same way, and a fair-coin null on 9 domain signs gives p = 2^-9 = 0.002 one-tailed. That number is about CONSISTENCY, not about effect size, and must not be quoted as significance for the 0.12 nat: the 9 domains are one model, one seed, one run per arm, so the arm delta is a common shift and the sign test's real n is 1 pair, not 9. A confidence statement about the magnitude needs seeds this A/B does not have. The honest summary is therefore asymmetric: 'no evidence zero-init helps, consistent weak evidence it hurts, magnitude unresolved'. That asymmetry is enough for the recipe decision (a change with no upside and a consistent downside sign does not go in) and NOT enough for a quantitative claim about the cost. MECHANISM NOTE, so the arm is not blamed for the wrong thing: the step-0 zero gradient is confirmed benign -- test_zero_init_out.py measured w13 1.56e-02 / qkv 7.02e-03 at step 1, so no branch stayed dead, and the pod's own run printed 'zero_init_out: 24 output projections zeroed' (24 = 2 x 12 layers). The arm really was the arm. gnorm tells the story: 54.85 at step 10 against baseline 1.71, i.e. the identity start makes the first real update violent rather than gentle, and by step 300 gnorm is still 1.44 vs 0.87. What zero-init bought was a delayed and rougher start, not a smoother one.
- **So:** zero_init_out does NOT enter the recipe (1e concurs, 2026-09-02 18:0xZ). Flag stays in train.py/model.py, default False, guarded by scripts/test_zero_init_out.py -- it costs nothing off and re-running it at another scale is one flag. NOT re-run with seeds: resolving a 0.12 nat effect at sigma=0.0516 needs roughly 4+4 seeds (~8 x 1300s x 2 cards) to buy a number whose only use is quantifying a change we are not adopting. If zero-init ever becomes load-bearing for another reason, that is when the seeds get spent.

### ab_shapelr — SHAPELR WINS ON DIRECTION, FLAT ON MAGNITUDE -- the mirror image of A/B (3). domain_loss unweighted mean 2.848 -> 2.787, -0.0611 nat (0.25x the 0.24 bar, 1.18 sigma at sigma=0.0516); BETTER IN 9 OF 9 DOMAINS, per-domain -0.0396 (code_py_starcoder) .. -0.0855 (chatml), no domain reaching the bar. Train/val, smoke only: train 2.332 -> 2.279, step500 val 2.620 -> 2.569, ep1 val 2.603 -> 2.552 (-0.051 on both val readings). Wall 1321s -> 1219s, so the arm was also 7.7% faster, unexplained and NOT claimed as a speed result (different process, and the base arm ran under a card-4 pytest for part of its window).

minimal_pairs IS IDENTICAL TO 16 DIGITS ON BOTH ARMS -- 0.7148014440433214 -- and that is a coincidence of integer arithmetic, not a re-read of one checkpoint. 1e asked for this to be excluded and it is, three ways. (a) Per-dimension correct counts DIFFER on all five: classifier 33->35, factual 10->13, function_word 19->18, numeric 70->65, word_order 66->67; the deltas +2+3-1-5+1 sum to exactly 0, so both arms score 198/277 = 0.7148014440433214 while disagreeing on 12 individual pairs. (b) mean_margin_nats differs at 1e-4: 1.4447211737236823 vs 1.4448631506964618. (c) domain_loss differs on every one of nine domains. A re-read of the same checkpoint would agree in ALL of those, not only in the total. The identical overall is therefore real and uninformative -- with n=277 and 5 dimensions the accuracy is a ratio of small integers, and equal totals from unequal parts is an ordinary outcome, not a signal.
- **Asked:** A/B (2a) speedrun record: Muon lr x max(1, out/in)^0.5 per parameter shape. Muon orthogonalises its update, so every column of X carries roughly unit RMS whatever the shape -- a tall (fan-out) matrix therefore takes a per-element step that is small relative to its weight, and the factor restores it. MEASURED AFFECTED SET on this exact config (d=1024, L=12, heads=8, ffn_hidden=3072): 36 of 63 Muon tensors, factors kv_up [2048,256] 2.8284, w13 [6144,1024] 2.4495, qkv [3072,1024] 1.7321, qg [2048,1024] 1.4142, gb [1040,1024] 1.0078; o, w2 and kv_down get exactly 1.0 from the clamp. PREDICTION: kv_up carries the largest factor of anything in the model (MLA latent is d/4, so it is the tallest by ratio) and is where an instability would appear first -- watch its weight norm, not just the loss. This arm is 2a ONLY: NorMuon (beta2 0.9) and the momentum-to-0.85 tail are a SECOND arm, deliberately not bundled, because three changes in one arm cannot say which one moved the loss. READING: domain_loss (0.24 nat) + minimal_pairs (11.5pt) against NOISE_THRESHOLDS.
- **Learned:** FLAT, NOT FLOOR, direction favourable -- and the same asymmetry as (3) with the sign reversed. The instrument resolves 0.24 nat; this effect is 0.061, so the pre-registered rule says it is not a result in either direction. 9/9 domains agreeing gives p = 2^-9 = 0.002 on a fair-coin null, which describes the CONSISTENCY of the reading and NOT the effect size: one model, one seed, one run per arm, so the arm delta is a common shift and the sign test's real n is 1 pair, not 9. No quantitative claim about the 0.061 nat.

SAME BORROWED-SIGMA CAVEAT AS (3), and here it cuts the other way, which is the point worth keeping: the 0.24 bar comes from ds.seed_variance_0p2b, four 0.2b runs (world 7, mix_scale_0.2b, warmup 20, fp8 True). This A/B read at 0.26B tokens on world 4 -- 1.31x the bar's tokens, closer than (3)'s 1.64x, but still borrowed. A too-wide bar makes FLAT too easy, so for a verdict of flat the borrowed sigma is anti-conservative in BOTH experiments: in (3) it may have masked a real cost, here it may be masking a real benefit. The bar is 4.65 sigma, so -0.0611 would clear it once sigma < 0.0131 -- sigma would have to fall to a QUARTER of the measured 0.0516, which is a bigger stretch than (3)'s halving. Flat is the safer read here.

WHAT THIS SAYS ABOUT THE HYPOTHESIS. The pre-registered prediction named kv_up as where an instability would appear first (largest factor, 2.8284, since the MLA latent is d/4). No instability appeared: gnorm ran 5.18 at step100 / 1.65 at 200 / 0.54 at 300 / 0.64 at 400 / 0.09 at 500, against base 2.92/2.40/0.87/0.69/0.11 -- higher early, converging, never diverging. So the arm is cheap and consistently mildly positive, which is exactly the profile that a 500-step A/B cannot separate from zero and a longer run might.
- **So:** muon_shape_lr is a CANDIDATE, not adopted and not rejected -- the opposite disposition from zero_init_out, on the same magnitude of evidence, because the sign is reversed and the cost is nil. Flag stays in train.py, default False, guarded by scripts/test_muon_shape_lr.py. RE-ASK AT THE NEXT LADDER POINT rather than spending seeds here: at 3.24B tokens the same relative effect would be larger in absolute nats and the ladder's own checkpoints give the comparison free, which is the same reasoning that deferred the KDA A/B (ds.attn_ab_read_protocol). Spending 4+4 seeds at 0.26B to resolve 0.061 nat buys a number for a decision that the ladder answers for free. NOT bundled with NorMuon or the momentum tail -- those remain a separate arm, per the original hypothesis.
